24 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. LJAN.-MAR, (Received 22nd April 1901.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KIAOCHOW, 15TH APRIL 1901. SIR, L.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circulars Nos. 21 of 1869, 14 of 1872, and 6 of 1875, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade of this port for the first quarter of the year 190r. b. The Custom House (M X I) was opened on the ist July 1899. The collection of Tariff Import and Export Duty and Convention Likin on Opium (Hk.!7!&amp;amp; 30 + 8o per picul), and the collection of Native Duties and Likin on Junk Cargoes at half Tariff rates, commenced on that day. The stations at present provisionally controlled by the Kiaochow Customs are known as Taputon ( f ); Hungsbihyai (j ( g); Lingshanwei (a al g), including Tachiankou (A t@ J); Nuku ( 3 ); Tsangko (g 1); and Shatszkou (i ) including Tengyao (J 2). c. The word &quot;Inwards&quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot;Outwards,&quot; from China, the statistics of goods landed and consumed in German territory not being included. PART IV. NOTIFICATIONS, During the Quarter ended March 31, 1901.
222 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. JAN.-MAR., NOTICES TO MARINERS. CHINA SEA. No. 356. YANGTZE RIVER CHINKIANG DISTRICT. NORTH TREE ROCKS BUOY. REFERRING to Notice to Mariners No. 123 (Special), dated the 26th December igoo, notice is hereby giveli that the red buoy; formerly moored to the south-westward of the North Tree Rceks, has been removed, and a black conical buoy has been moored about 3 cables to the east-north-eastward of the rocks. NoTE. Owing to the washing away of the left, or north-eastern, bank of the river in this vicinity, the distance between the North Tree Rocks and- this bank, which was about 330 yards in April 1882, is now about 2,400 yards. The Johnson Flats have also extended so much to the north-eastward that there is no longer a safe channel to tle south-westward of the rocks. SHANGHAI, 9th February 190. No. 357. T IENTSI ND ISTR CT. INTENDED ALTERATION TO TSAO-FEI-TIEN LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that in or about April next the present sixth-order fixed light at this station will be replaced by a group-flashing light of the fourth order, showing two white flashes in quick succession every 20 seconds. The old light will be continued till the new one is lighted. SHANGHAI, x3th Febrary io.
1901.] NOTIFICXATIONs. 223 No. 358. YANGTZE RIVER-CHINKIANG DISTRICT. ESTABLISHMENT OF A LIGHJT-BOAT AT ROSINA ROCK. NOTICE is hereby given that a light-boat, showing a red light, which in clear weather should be visible at a distance of about 4 miles, has been moored about 2 cables to the southward of Rosina Rock. Vessels bound up river should pass the light-boat on their starboard hand at a distance of not less than I cable. SHiNGHAI, 14th February 1901. No. 359. YANGTZE RIVER-KIUKIANG DISTRICT. SHENSI POINT BEACON-LIGHT ESTABLISHED. NOTICE is hereby given that the Shensi Point Beacon-light was exhibited for the firet time at sunset on the 7th ultimo. The illuminating apparatus is &amp;lt;ioptric, of the sixth order, showing a fixed white light. SHANGilAI, 21d Acrc 1901. No. 360. YANGTZE RIVER-KI UKIANG D STRICTI BUCKMINSTER BEACON-LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Buckminster Island Beacon-light, No. 74in the published List for 19o, has been removed from Chin-te-chow.Island and placed at T&#39;u-ch&#39;iao, on the left (western) bank of the river, just below the mouth of the creek, and that it will in future be known as the Buckminster Beacon-light. SHANGHAI, 2nd March iCoi.
224 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. [AN.-MAR., 1901.1 No. 361. YANGTZE RIVER-CHINKIANG DISTRICT. MUD FORT BEACON-LIGHT. NOTICE is hereby given that the Mud Fort Beacon-light, has been shifted 370 yards N. 8io E. from its last position, in consequence of the washing away of the river bank. SHANGHAI, 2ndlMarch igoi. No. 362. SHANGHAI DISTRICT. SOUTHERN ENTRANCE TO THE YANGTZE. CHANGE IN POSITION OF KIUTOAN (SURVEYING) BEACON. N rICE is hereby given that, owing to the washing away of the river bank, it has been found necessary to remove the Kiutoan (Surveying) Beacon, No. 44 in the published List for zgoi, and that it has been replaced by a pyramidal, open-work structure of wood, 7o feet high, standing 68o feet S. 87* W., magnetic, from the former site. SHANGHAI, 9th March 1901. No. 363. YANGTZE RIVER--KIUKANG D ISTR ICT. TUNGLIU ISLAND BEACON-LIGHT ESTABLISHED. NOTICE is hereby given that the Tungliu Island Beacon-light was exhibited for the first time at sunset on the 16th ultimo. The illuminating apparatus is dioptric, of the sixth order, showing a fixed white light. The beacon is placed on the lower, or northern, end of the island. SHANGHAI, 16th March 1901.

APPENDIX I. KOWLOON AND LAPPA: QUARTERLY RETURNS OF TRADE. JANUARY-MARCH 901.
240 CUSTOMS GAZETTE. JAN.-MAR., (Beceived 22nd April got.) CUSTOM HOUSE, KOWLOON, i5TH APILL 1901. SIR, I.-a. In conformity with the instructions contained in your Circular No. 381, Second Series, I now transmit to the Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring stations in Native vessels for the first quarter of the year 1901. b. The stations at present controlled by the Kowloon Customs are Taishan ( N ), Lintin ( fT and Shamchun ( JJj), on the north and west; Samun (2 Jf), on the east; and Shaiichung ( ) and Shatowkok (W Pf A), on the Mirs Bay littoral. The Custom House is called R f N. c. The word &quot;Inwards &quot; in these statistics means to China, and the word &quot; Outwards,&quot; from China. II.
